Two identical subunits often form a functional unit, with each contributing to the active site. This is the most typical arrangement for enzymes of this class.<br>- **Monomer (C):** While some oxidoreductases are monomeric, the presence of interfacial residues and the domain architecture here suggest subunit-subunit interactions.<br>- **Homotetramer (D):** Less common for this size and architecture; tetrameric arrangements are more typical of larger enzymes or those requiring multiple active sites.<br><br>Given the Rossmann fold, the domain architecture, and the typical quaternary structures of bacterial oxidoreductases with this organization, **homodimer** is the most supported and most commonly observed arrangement.<br><br>2. answer: B</td>

<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which assignment correctly identifies the residue positions involved in this protein’s heme-binding site?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Axial heme-iron binding: Y32 and C52; covalent heme attachment: C55 and H56.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Axial heme-iron binding: Y32 and H56; covalent heme attachment: C52 and C55.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Axial heme-iron binding: C52 and C55; covalent heme attachment: Y32 and H56.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Axial heme-iron binding: H56 and C55; covalent heme attachment: Y32 and C52.<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>First, I need to identify the key residues at the specified positions in the protein sequence:<br>- Position 32: Y (Tyrosine)<br>- Position 52: C (Cysteine)<br>- Position 55: C (Cysteine)<br>- Position 56: H (Histidine)<br><br>The sequence around positions 52-56 is: **C52-A53-N54-C55-H56**, which is a **CxxCH motif** (Cys-X-X-Cys-His). This is the canonical heme-binding motif of **cytochrome c-type** proteins.<br><br>In cytochrome c-type proteins, the heme-binding mechanism is well established:<br>- The **two cysteine residues** (C52 and C55) form **covalent thioether bonds** with the heme vinyl groups — this is the covalent attachment.<br>- The **histidine** (H56) from the CxxCH motif serves as one **axial ligand** to the iron center.<br>- The **second axial ligand** is typically methionine, but in certain cytochrome c-type proteins (e.g., cytochrome c peroxidase, some plant cytochromes c6), a **tyrosine** substitutes for methionine as the second axial ligand. Y32 would serve this role.<br><br>Evaluating each option:<br>- **A**: Incorrect — assigns C52 as axial and H56 as covalent, which contradicts the known chemistry of the CxxCH motif.<br>- **B**: Correct — Y32 and H56 as axial iron ligands; C52 and C55 for covalent thioether attachment. This matches the canonical cytochrome c-type binding mechanism.<br>- **C**: Incorrect — reverses the roles entirely.<br>- **D**: Incorrect — assigns C55 as axial and Y32 as covalent, which is biochemically nonsensical.<br><br>2. answer: B</td>
